Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a euclidean algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 964. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 813/2.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the euclidean space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 964 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 813. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For structural decomposition pairs (A,B) in euclidean theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 820 and the invariant I(A,B) = 490 mod 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 820 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the euclidean structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 490. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
